Well, um... control is a strong word, isn't it? I mean, I try my best to keep things sensible, I really do. But...

Well, um... I suppose "regularly" is another one of those tricky words, isn't it? I mean, I certainly don't plan for it to happen often, and I try so hard to keep things normal. But... 

I actually started keeping a little notebook, just a small one, to track things. I thought it might help me be a bit more disciplined. But looking at it... well, it seems that for quite a few months now, almost every week has had at least one of those afternoons where things just... slip away from me. 

And, um... I haven't mentioned this part yet, and I'm so terribly embarrassed to even say it out loud... but after it happens, I just feel so awful, so full and guilty, that I... I try to fix it. Sometimes I force myself to be sick, or I take laxatives... or I just won't eat anything at all the next day. Other times I'll push myself to walk for hours, or do heavy yard chores until I'm exhausted, just to try and undo what I did. 

I know it sounds dreadful, especially for a man my age. I should know better, I really should. I'm so sorry to lay all this on you. Is... is that the kind of information you were looking for? I hope I'm not being too much of a bother...

Is that the sort of thing you can help with?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... guilty? I'm not entirely sure, Doctor. I haven't really thought about it using that word, to be honest with you. \n\nI mean, I suppose nobody likes overdoing things, but... I haven't really noticed a strong feeling of guilt, if that makes sense? I hope I'm answering your question right. I'm sorry if that's not very helpful... did you mean feeling bad about wasting the food, or something else?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose \"large\" is a bit subjective, isn't it? I mean, I certainly don't mean to eat a lot, and I'm sure it's just me being a bit careless sometimes... \n\nBut, yes, I guess on those afternoons when I'm all alone at the house, things can get a little out of hand. I might sit down and... well, before I even really look up or realize what I'm doing, I can go through a whole loaf of bread. And maybe some peanut butter, and a few cookies... whatever leftovers are in the fridge... and maybe some ice cream, too. All in one sitting. \n\nI know that sounds terrible, and I'm so sorry, I really shouldn't be doing that at my age. Does that sound like what you were asking about, Doctor? I hope I'm not making things sound worse than they are...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating when you have those episodes where you consume a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Well, um... control is a strong word, isn't it? I mean, I try my best to keep things sensible, I really do. But... \n\nI think I mentioned this earlier, and I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, but... yes, that's actually the part that frightens me a bit. Once I start, it's... it's almost like I'm watching myself do it. My hands just keep grabbing for the next thing, the next bite. And the whole time, I'm sitting there in my own head saying, 'Tom, you need to stop this right now, this is ridiculous.' But I just... I can't seem to make myself stop until it's all gone. \n\nI know that must sound so weak, and I'm really embarrassed to even admit it out loud. I should be able to just put the fork down, shouldn't I? I hope you don't think too badly of me for it... is that the sort of thing you meant by losing control?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food interfere with your daily life or cause you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I try very hard to keep it from being a bother to anyone else. I don't want to be a nuisance, you know. But... I suppose, if I'm being entirely honest—and I hope I don't sound like I'm just feeling sorry for myself—it has changed things a little bit. \n\nI used to be quite active with a local garden group, but I've... well, I've sort of pulled away from them lately. And the family meals, too, I've been skipping those. It's just... I find myself either planning my whole day around the food, or I'm just so terribly ashamed afterward. I worry so much that someone will look at me and just... notice what I'm doing, or what I've done. \n\nI feel awful missing out on seeing my family, I really do. I know I should be stronger than this at my age. Does that answer your question, Doctor? I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need...\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen to you regularly?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose \"regularly\" is another one of those tricky words, isn't it? I mean, I certainly don't plan for it to happen often, and I try so hard to keep things normal. But... \n\nI actually started keeping a little notebook, just a small one, to track things. I thought it might help me be a bit more disciplined. But looking at it... well, it seems that for quite a few months now, almost every week has had at least one of those afternoons where things just... slip away from me. \n\nAnd, um... I haven't mentioned this part yet, and I'm so terribly embarrassed to even say it out loud... but after it happens, I just feel so awful, so full and guilty, that I... I try to fix it. Sometimes I force myself to be sick, or I take laxatives... or I just won't eat anything at all the next day. Other times I'll push myself to walk for hours, or do heavy yard chores until I'm exhausted, just to try and undo what I did. \n\nI know it sounds dreadful, especially for a man my age. Even while I’m sitting there telling myself, you know, 'Tom, stop this right now,' it's like I just keep going. \n\nI hope I'm explaining that okay? Is that the sort of thing you can help with?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... guilty? I'm not entirely sure, Doctor. I haven't really thought about it using that word, to be honest with you. \n\nI mean, I suppose nobody likes overdoing things, but... I haven't really noticed a strong feeling of guilt, if that makes sense? I hope I'm answering your question right. I'm sorry if that's not very helpful... did you mean feeling bad about wasting the food, or something else?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose \"large\" is a bit subjective, isn't it? I mean, I certainly don't mean to eat a lot, and I'm sure it's just me being a bit careless sometimes... \n\nBut, yes, I guess on those afternoons when I'm all alone at the house, things can get a little out of hand. I might sit down and... well, before I even really look up or realize what I'm doing, I can go through a whole loaf of bread. And maybe some peanut butter, and a few cookies... whatever leftovers are in the fridge... and maybe some ice cream, too. All in one sitting. \n\nI know that sounds terrible, and I'm so sorry, I really shouldn't be doing that at my age. Does that sound like what you were asking about, Doctor? I hope I'm not making things sound worse than they are...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating when you have those episodes where you consume a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Well, um... control is a strong word, isn't it? I mean, I try my best to keep things sensible, I really do. But... \n\nI think I mentioned this earlier, and I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, but... yes, that's actually the part that frightens me a bit. Once I start, it's... it's almost like I'm watching myself do it. My hands just keep grabbing for the next thing, the next bite. And the whole time, I'm sitting there in my own head saying, 'Tom, you need to stop this right now, this is ridiculous.' But I just... I can't seem to make myself stop until it's all gone. \n\nI know that must sound so weak, and I'm really embarrassed to even admit it out loud. I should be able to just put the fork down, shouldn't I? I hope you don't think too badly of me for it... is that the sort of thing you meant by losing control?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food interfere with your daily life or cause you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I try very hard to keep it from being a bother to anyone else. I don't want to be a nuisance, you know. But... I suppose, if I'm being entirely honest—and I hope I don't sound like I'm just feeling sorry for myself—it has changed things a little bit. \n\nI used to be quite active with a local garden group, but I've... well, I've sort of pulled away from them lately. And the family meals, too, I've been skipping those. It's just... I find myself either planning my whole day around the food, or I'm just so terribly ashamed afterward. I worry so much that someone will look at me and just... notice what I'm doing, or what I've done. \n\nI feel awful missing out on seeing my family, I really do. I know I should be stronger than this at my age. Does that answer your question, Doctor? I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need...\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen to you regularly?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose \"regularly\" is another one of those tricky words, isn't it? I mean, I certainly don't plan for it to happen often, and I try so hard to keep things normal. But... \n\nI actually started keeping a little notebook, just a small one, to track things. I thought it might help me be a bit more disciplined. But looking at it... well, it seems that for quite a few months now, almost every week has had at least one of those afternoons where things just... slip away from me. \n\nAnd, um... I haven't mentioned this part yet, and I'm so terribly embarrassed to even say it out loud... but after it happens, I just feel so awful, so full and guilty, that I... I try to fix it. Sometimes I force myself to be sick, or I take laxatives... or I just won't eat anything at all the next day. Other times I'll push myself to walk for hours, or do heavy yard chores until I'm exhausted, just to try and undo what I did. \n\nI know it sounds dreadful, especially for a man my age. I should know better, I really should. I'm so sorry to lay all this on you. Is... is that the kind of information you were looking for? I hope I'm not being too much of a bother...\n\nDoctor: How often do you try to fix it after one of those episodes by making yourself sick, taking laxatives, or restricting food the next day?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose it goes right along with what I was just mentioning about my little notebook. I really wish I could say it was just a rare slip-up, I really do. But... when I actually look at what I've written down in the logs, it shows that for many months now... well, nearly every week has had at least one afternoon where I eat out of control, and then... then I try to make up for it right after. \n\nIt's almost always tied together like that. At least once a week, I'll have the episode and then do the vomiting, or take the laxatives, or do the fasting... or push myself to the limit with the walking and the yard chores. \n\nI'm so terribly ashamed to admit that to you. I really thought I was doing better than the notebook says I am. I hope you don't think I'm a completely hopeless case, a man my age acting like this... Is... is that what you needed to know, Doctor? I want to make sure I'm answering you properly.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Oh, dear... um... just the... the making myself sick part? I... I'm afraid I don't know if I can give you an exact number for just that one thing, to be perfectly honest with you.

I try so very hard not to do that one, and I suppose I just didn't want to see it written out in ink on the page. \n\nSo I'm not entirely sure how often it's that specifically, compared to the walking or the fasting. But... well, I guess what scares me most is that sometimes, once I start eating, it just feels like my hands keep reaching for more. Even while I’m sitting there telling myself, you know, 'Tom, stop this right now,' it's like I just keep going. \n\nI hope I'm explaining that okay? Is that the sort of thing you can help with?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... guilty? I'm not entirely sure, Doctor. I haven't really thought about it using that word, to be honest with you. \n\nI mean, I suppose nobody likes overdoing things, but... I haven't really noticed a strong feeling of guilt, if that makes sense? I hope I'm answering your question right. I'm sorry if that's not very helpful... did you mean feeling bad about wasting the food, or something else?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose \"large\" is a bit subjective, isn't it? I mean, I certainly don't mean to eat a lot, and I'm sure it's just me being a bit careless sometimes... \n\nBut, yes, I guess on those afternoons when I'm all alone at the house, things can get a little out of hand. I might sit down and... well, before I even really look up or realize what I'm doing, I can go through a whole loaf of bread. And maybe some peanut butter, and a few cookies... whatever leftovers are in the fridge... and maybe some ice cream, too. All in one sitting. \n\nI know that sounds terrible, and I'm so sorry, I really shouldn't be doing that at my age. Does that sound like what you were asking about, Doctor? I hope I'm not making things sound worse than they are...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating when you have those episodes where you consume a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Well, um... control is a strong word, isn't it? I mean, I try my best to keep things sensible, I really do. But... \n\nI think I mentioned this earlier, and I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, but... yes, that's actually the part that frightens me a bit. Once I start, it's... it's almost like I'm watching myself do it. My hands just keep grabbing for the next thing, the next bite. And the whole time, I'm sitting there in my own head saying, 'Tom, you need to stop this right now, this is ridiculous.' But I just... I can't seem to make myself stop until it's all gone. \n\nI know that must sound so weak, and I'm really embarrassed to even admit it out loud. I should be able to just put the fork down, shouldn't I? I hope you don't think too badly of me for it... is that the sort of thing you meant by losing control?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food interfere with your daily life or cause you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I try very hard to keep it from being a bother to anyone else. I don't want to be a nuisance, you know. But... I suppose, if I'm being entirely honest—and I hope I don't sound like I'm just feeling sorry for myself—it has changed things a little bit. \n\nI used to be quite active with a local garden group, but I've... well, I've sort of pulled away from them lately. And the family meals, too, I've been skipping those. It's just... I find myself either planning my whole day around the food, or I'm just so terribly ashamed afterward. I worry so much that someone will look at me and just... notice what I'm doing, or what I've done. \n\nI feel awful missing out on seeing my family, I really do. I know I should be stronger than this at my age. Does that answer your question, Doctor? I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need...\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen to you regularly?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose \"regularly\" is another one of those tricky words, isn't it? I mean, I certainly don't plan for it to happen often, and I try so hard to keep things normal. But... \n\nI actually started keeping a little notebook, just a small one, to track things. I thought it might help me be a bit more disciplined. But looking at it... well, it seems that for quite a few months now, almost every week has had at least one of those afternoons where things just... slip away from me. \n\nAnd, um... I haven't mentioned this part yet, and I'm so terribly embarrassed to even say it out loud... but after it happens, I just feel so awful, so full and guilty, that I... I try to fix it. Sometimes I force myself to be sick, or I take laxatives... or I just won't eat anything at all the next day. Other times I'll push myself to walk for hours, or do heavy yard chores until I'm exhausted, just to try and undo what I did. \n\nI know it sounds dreadful, especially for a man my age. I should know better, I really should. I'm so sorry to lay all this on you. Is... is that the kind of information you were looking for? I hope I'm not being too much of a bother...\n\nDoctor: How often do you try to fix it after one of those episodes by making yourself sick, taking laxatives, or restricting food the next day?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose it goes right along with what I was just mentioning about my little notebook. I really wish I could say it was just a rare slip-up, I really do. But... when I actually look at what I've written down in the logs, it shows that for many months now... well, nearly every week has had at least one afternoon where I eat out of control, and then... then I try to make up for it right after. \n\nIt's almost always tied together like that. At least once a week, I'll have the episode and then do the vomiting, or take the laxatives, or do the fasting... or push myself to the limit with the walking and the yard chores. \n\nI'm so terribly ashamed to admit that to you. I really thought I was doing better than the notebook says I am. I hope you don't think I'm a completely hopeless case, a man my age acting like this... Is... is that what you needed to know, Doctor? I want to make sure I'm answering you properly.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself sick after one of those episodes of eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... um... just the... the making myself sick part? I... I'm afraid I don't know if I can give you an exact number for just that one thing, to be perfectly honest with you. \n\nIn my little notebook, I usually just put a... a little mark when I do *something* to try and fix the eating. I don't always write down exactly which thing I did, because... well, the... the throwing up is the part I feel the most dreadful about. It's so terribly undignified, you know? I try so very hard not to do that one, and I suppose I just didn't want to see it written out in ink on the page. \n\nSo I'm not entirely sure how often it's that specifically, compared to the walking or the fasting. I'm so sorry, I know I should have kept better track for you. Is... is it alright if I don't have the exact answer for that one? I hope I'm not making your job too difficult.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how many times in a month you make yourself sick after eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... um, a whole month? I... I really don't think I could give you a proper estimate, Doctor. I'm so sorry. \n\nLike I was saying, I just put a little check mark when I do *something* to try and make up for the eating. And I really do try to rely on the walking or the yard work instead, because... well, it feels a bit more productive, doesn't it? Less... less shameful. So the times I actually... make myself sick... I try to just push those out of my mind as quickly as possible. \n\nI wouldn't want to give you the wrong number and mislead you. I know you need accurate information to help me. I suppose it happens sometimes, but... I just couldn't say how many times in a month. I do apologize for being so unhelpful with this. Am I... am I making things terribly complicated for you by not knowing?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a large amount of food and then trying to compensate for it happen to you at least once a week for the past three months?\n\nPatient: Well, yes... yes, Doctor. I believe I mentioned that just a moment ago, but I know you have to be absolutely certain for your records. \n\nWhen I look at my little notebook, it does show that for many months now... certainly past the three-month mark... nearly every week has had at least one of those afternoons. You know, where I lose control with the food and then... then feel like I simply must do something to fix it right after, whether that's the fasting, or the chores, or... the other things. \n\nIt's... it's all written down right there in my logs. Oh, three times a week? Um... well, let me think for a moment. I... I don't believe it's quite that often, Doctor. 

I know my notebook has a mark almost every week, like I said, but... three times in one week sounds a bit... excessive, doesn't it? So, no, I don't think it reaches three times a week. It's usually just that one bad afternoon, maybe two if it's been a particularly difficult week, but... not three. I hope that's a better answer for you? I really am trying to be as accurate as I can be.

I mean, I certainly don't mean to eat a lot, and I'm sure it's just me being a bit careless sometimes... \n\nBut, yes, I guess on those afternoons when I'm all alone at the house, things can get a little out of hand. I might sit down and... well, before I even really look up or realize what I'm doing, I can go through a whole loaf of bread. And maybe some peanut butter, and a few cookies... whatever leftovers are in the fridge... and maybe some ice cream, too. All in one sitting. \n\nI know that sounds terrible, and I'm so sorry, I really shouldn't be doing that at my age. Does that sound like what you were asking about, Doctor? I hope I'm not making things sound worse than they are...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating when you have those episodes where you consume a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Well, um... control is a strong word, isn't it? I mean, I try my best to keep things sensible, I really do. But... \n\nI think I mentioned this earlier, and I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, but... yes, that's actually the part that frightens me a bit. Once I start, it's... it's almost like I'm watching myself do it. My hands just keep grabbing for the next thing, the next bite. And the whole time, I'm sitting there in my own head saying, 'Tom, you need to stop this right now, this is ridiculous.' But I just... I can't seem to make myself stop until it's all gone. \n\nI know that must sound so weak, and I'm really embarrassed to even admit it out loud. I should be able to just put the fork down, shouldn't I? I hope you don't think too badly of me for it... is that the sort of thing you meant by losing control?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food interfere with your daily life or cause you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I try very hard to keep it from being a bother to anyone else. I don't want to be a nuisance, you know. But... I suppose, if I'm being entirely honest—and I hope I don't sound like I'm just feeling sorry for myself—it has changed things a little bit. \n\nI used to be quite active with a local garden group, but I've... well, I've sort of pulled away from them lately. And the family meals, too, I've been skipping those. It's just... I find myself either planning my whole day around the food, or I'm just so terribly ashamed afterward. I worry so much that someone will look at me and just... notice what I'm doing, or what I've done. \n\nI feel awful missing out on seeing my family, I really do. I know I should be stronger than this at my age. Does that answer your question, Doctor? I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need...\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen to you regularly?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose \"regularly\" is another one of those tricky words, isn't it? I mean, I certainly don't plan for it to happen often, and I try so hard to keep things normal. But... \n\nI actually started keeping a little notebook, just a small one, to track things. I thought it might help me be a bit more disciplined. But looking at it... well, it seems that for quite a few months now, almost every week has had at least one of those afternoons where things just... slip away from me. \n\nAnd, um... I haven't mentioned this part yet, and I'm so terribly embarrassed to even say it out loud... but after it happens, I just feel so awful, so full and guilty, that I... I try to fix it. Sometimes I force myself to be sick, or I take laxatives... or I just won't eat anything at all the next day. Other times I'll push myself to walk for hours, or do heavy yard chores until I'm exhausted, just to try and undo what I did. \n\nI know it sounds dreadful, especially for a man my age. I should know better, I really should. I'm so sorry to lay all this on you. Is... is that the kind of information you were looking for? I hope I'm not being too much of a bother...\n\nDoctor: How often do you try to fix it after one of those episodes by making yourself sick, taking laxatives, or restricting food the next day?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose it goes right along with what I was just mentioning about my little notebook. I really wish I could say it was just a rare slip-up, I really do. But... when I actually look at what I've written down in the logs, it shows that for many months now... well, nearly every week has had at least one afternoon where I eat out of control, and then... then I try to make up for it right after. \n\nIt's almost always tied together like that. At least once a week, I'll have the episode and then do the vomiting, or take the laxatives, or do the fasting... or push myself to the limit with the walking and the yard chores. \n\nI'm so terribly ashamed to admit that to you. I really thought I was doing better than the notebook says I am. I hope you don't think I'm a completely hopeless case, a man my age acting like this... Is... is that what you needed to know, Doctor? I want to make sure I'm answering you properly.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself sick after one of those episodes of eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... um... just the... the making myself sick part? I... I'm afraid I don't know if I can give you an exact number for just that one thing, to be perfectly honest with you. \n\nIn my little notebook, I usually just put a... a little mark when I do *something* to try and fix the eating. I don't always write down exactly which thing I did, because... well, the... the throwing up is the part I feel the most dreadful about. It's so terribly undignified, you know? I try so very hard not to do that one, and I suppose I just didn't want to see it written out in ink on the page. \n\nSo I'm not entirely sure how often it's that specifically, compared to the walking or the fasting. I'm so sorry, I know I should have kept better track for you. Is... is it alright if I don't have the exact answer for that one? I hope I'm not making your job too difficult.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how many times in a month you make yourself sick after eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... um, a whole month? I... I really don't think I could give you a proper estimate, Doctor. I'm so sorry. \n\nLike I was saying, I just put a little check mark when I do *something* to try and make up for the eating. And I really do try to rely on the walking or the yard work instead, because... well, it feels a bit more productive, doesn't it? Less... less shameful. So the times I actually... make myself sick... I try to just push those out of my mind as quickly as possible. \n\nI wouldn't want to give you the wrong number and mislead you. I know you need accurate information to help me. I suppose it happens sometimes, but... I just couldn't say how many times in a month. I do apologize for being so unhelpful with this. Am I... am I making things terribly complicated for you by not knowing?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a large amount of food and then trying to compensate for it happen to you at least once a week for the past three months?\n\nPatient: Well, yes... yes, Doctor. I believe I mentioned that just a moment ago, but I know you have to be absolutely certain for your records. \n\nWhen I look at my little notebook, it does show that for many months now... certainly past the three-month mark... nearly every week has had at least one of those afternoons. You know, where I lose control with the food and then... then feel like I simply must do something to fix it right after, whether that's the fasting, or the chores, or... the other things. \n\nIt's... it's all written down right there in my logs. I hope it's alright that I'm repeating myself, I just want to be sure I'm giving you exactly what you need to hear. Did I answer that properly for you this time?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a large amount of food and then trying to compensate for it happen to you at least three times a week?\n\nPatient: Oh, three times a week? Um... well, let me think for a moment. I... I don't believe it's quite that often, Doctor. \n\nI know my notebook has a mark almost every week, like I said, but... three times in one week sounds a bit... excessive, doesn't it? I really do try my best to keep things under control, and I wouldn't want you to think I'm just completely falling apart every other day. I'm usually quite good at keeping busy with my yard work and staying out of the kitchen. \n\nSo, no, I don't think it reaches three times a week. It's usually just that one bad afternoon, maybe two if it's been a particularly difficult week, but... not three. I hope that's a better answer for you? I really am trying to be as accurate as I can be.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your episodes of eating a large amount of food and then trying to compensate for it have been occurring at the same frequency over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Oh, the frequency... um... well, I suppose it's been fairly steady, Doctor. \n\nI haven't noticed any sudden changes, if that's what you mean. It's just sort of... been this way for a while now. I know I mentioned my little notebook, but I don't really tally things up at the end of the month or anything like that. I just try to take each week as it comes and do my best. \n\nSo, yes, I'd say it's been about the same over these past three months. Not getting worse, certainly! I really do try to keep a lid on it. Does that... does that answer your question the way you needed? I want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these episodes of eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh... well... I suppose anyone might feel a little silly after eating more than they meant to, right? \n\nBut guilty... I don't know if I'd use that exact word, Doctor. I mean, I do try to fix it right away, like I said, with the yard work or... the other things, so I make sure it doesn't cause any real harm. I just sort of dust myself off and get back to my day. I wouldn't want you to think I'm sitting around wallowing in misery or anything like that. I like to stay positive!\n\nI just... I just try to handle it quietly and move on. Is... is that a normal way to feel about it? I hope I'm not giving you the wrong impression.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel regret or self-criticism after eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Regret? Um... well, I try not to be too hard on myself, Doctor. I've always believed there's no use crying over spilled milk, you know? \n\nIf I happen to eat a bit more than I should have, I just... I make it right, like I mentioned before, and then I put it out of my mind. I certainly don't sit around calling myself names or getting all critical. I like to think I keep a pretty level head about these things. At my age, you learn you just have to take things in stride and focus on doing better the next day. \n\nI think it's important to stay positive and not beat yourself up, don't you? I hope that's the kind of attitude you're looking for from me. I really am trying to handle it all as sensibly as I can.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I suppose, if I'm being entirely honest—and I hope I don't sound like I'm just feeling sorry for myself—it has changed things a little bit. \n\nI used to be quite active with a local garden group, but I've... well, I've sort of pulled away from them lately. Other times I'll push myself to walk for hours, or do heavy yard chores until I'm exhausted, just to try and undo what I did. \n\nI know it sounds dreadful, especially for a man my age. I should know better, I really should. I'm so sorry to lay all this on you. Is... is that the kind of information you were looking for? I hope I'm not being too much of a bother...\n\nDoctor: How often do you try to fix it after one of those episodes by making yourself sick, taking laxatives, or restricting food the next day?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose it goes right along with what I was just mentioning about my little notebook. I really wish I could say it was just a rare slip-up, I really do. At least once a week, I'll have the episode and then do the vomiting, or take the laxatives, or do the fasting... or push myself to the limit with the walking and the yard chores. \n\nI'm so terribly ashamed to admit that to you. Um... well, I try not to be too hard on myself, Doctor. I've always believed there's no use crying over spilled milk, you know? \n\nIf I happen to eat a bit more than I should have, I just... I make it right, like I mentioned before, and then I put it out of my mind. I certainly don't sit around calling myself names or getting all critical. I like to think I keep a pretty level head about these things. At my age, you learn you just have to take things in stride and focus on doing better the next day. \n\nI think it's important to stay positive and not beat yourself up, don't you? I hope that's the kind of attitude you're looking for from me. I really am trying to handle it all as sensibly as I can.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had episodes where you ate a large amount of food in a short period of time without trying to compensate for it afterwards?\n\nPatient: Oh, without trying to make up for it? Um... no, Doctor, I don't believe so. \n\nI mean, I wouldn't just... leave it like that. If I slip up and have a bit too much, I always make sure to take care of it right away. It wouldn't be very responsible of me to just ignore it, would it? I always try to be very diligent about keeping things balanced. \n\nI've never just... let it go. I want to make sure I'm doing the right thing, you know? Keeping everything neat and tidy. Does that make sense? I hope that shows I'm trying my best to stay on top of things.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you ate a large amount of food in a short period of time without doing anything to try and compensate for it afterwards, such as vomiting, taking laxatives, or fasting?\n\nPatient: No... no, I really don't think I have. \n\nLike I was saying, I—I wouldn't just sit there and do nothing after... after overdoing it. That just wouldn't feel right to me. If I make a little mistake with my eating, I always make sure to fix it right away, whether that's taking a little something to help things along or... or just skipping my meals the next day to balance the ledger, so to speak. \n\nI like to think of it as just taking responsibility for my actions, you know? I wouldn't want you to think I'm the sort of person who just lets himself go completely. I always try to keep things under control. I hope that's the right way to handle it... is it? I just want to make sure I'm doing what I'm supposed to do.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had an episode where you ate a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling the need to do something to compensate for it afterwards?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 78 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, Doctor, I don't... I don't think I've ever felt quite like that. 

I mean, if I've had a bit too much, the feeling that I need to... to set things right, it's always there. It's just a natural reaction, I suppose. It would feel terribly careless of me to just go on with my day and not try to fix it. I wouldn't want to be the kind of person who doesn't care about the consequences, you know? 

I always feel that urge to balance things out. It's just my way of being responsible, I think. I hope you can understand that... I just want to make sure I'm keeping everything in order. That's a good thing, isn't it?

But... well, I guess what scares me most is that sometimes, once I start eating, it just feels like my hands keep reaching for more. Even while I’m sitting there telling myself, you know, 'Tom, stop this right now,' it's like I just keep going. \n\nI hope I'm explaining that okay? Is that the sort of thing you can help with?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... guilty? I'm not entirely sure, Doctor. I haven't really noticed a strong feeling of guilt, if that makes sense? I hope I'm answering your question right. I suppose \"large\" is a bit subjective, isn't it? I mean, I try my best to keep things sensible, I really do. But... \n\nI think I mentioned this earlier, and I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, but... yes, that's actually the part that frightens me a bit. Once I start, it's... it's almost like I'm watching myself do it. My hands just keep grabbing for the next thing, the next bite. And the whole time, I'm sitting there in my own head saying, 'Tom, you need to stop this right now, this is ridiculous.' But I just... I can't seem to make myself stop until it's all gone. \n\nI know that must sound so weak, and I'm really embarrassed to even admit it out loud. I should be able to just put the fork down, shouldn't I? I try very hard to keep it from being a bother to anyone else. I don't want to be a nuisance, you know. But... I suppose, if I'm being entirely honest—and I hope I don't sound like I'm just feeling sorry for myself—it has changed things a little bit. \n\nI used to be quite active with a local garden group, but I've... well, I've sort of pulled away from them lately. And the family meals, too, I've been skipping those. It's just... I find myself either planning my whole day around the food, or I'm just so terribly ashamed afterward. I worry so much that someone will look at me and just... notice what I'm doing, or what I've done. \n\nI feel awful missing out on seeing my family, I really do. I know I should be stronger than this at my age. Does that answer your question, Doctor? I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need...\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen to you regularly?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose \"regularly\" is another one of those tricky words, isn't it? I mean, I certainly don't plan for it to happen often, and I try so hard to keep things normal. But... \n\nI actually started keeping a little notebook, just a small one, to track things. I thought it might help me be a bit more disciplined. But looking at it... well, it seems that for quite a few months now, almost every week has had at least one of those afternoons where things just... slip away from me. \n\nAnd, um... I haven't mentioned this part yet, and I'm so terribly embarrassed to even say it out loud... but after it happens, I just feel so awful, so full and guilty, that I... I try to fix it. Sometimes I force myself to be sick, or I take laxatives... or I just won't eat anything at all the next day. Other times I'll push myself to walk for hours, or do heavy yard chores until I'm exhausted, just to try and undo what I did. \n\nI know it sounds dreadful, especially for a man my age. I should know better, I really should. I'm so sorry to lay all this on you. Is... is that the kind of information you were looking for? I hope I'm not being too much of a bother...\n\nDoctor: How often do you try to fix it after one of those episodes by making yourself sick, taking laxatives, or restricting food the next day?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose it goes right along with what I was just mentioning about my little notebook. I really wish I could say it was just a rare slip-up, I really do. But... when I actually look at what I've written down in the logs, it shows that for many months now... well, nearly every week has had at least one afternoon where I eat out of control, and then... then I try to make up for it right after. \n\nIt's almost always tied together like that. At least once a week, I'll have the episode and then do the vomiting, or take the laxatives, or do the fasting... or push myself to the limit with the walking and the yard chores. \n\nI'm so terribly ashamed to admit that to you. I really thought I was doing better than the notebook says I am. I hope you don't think I'm a completely hopeless case, a man my age acting like this... Is... is that what you needed to know, Doctor? I want to make sure I'm answering you properly.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself sick after one of those episodes of eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... um... just the... the making myself sick part? I... I'm afraid I don't know if I can give you an exact number for just that one thing, to be perfectly honest with you. \n\nIn my little notebook, I usually just put a... a little mark when I do *something* to try and fix the eating. I don't always write down exactly which thing I did, because... well, the... the throwing up is the part I feel the most dreadful about. It's so terribly undignified, you know? I try so very hard not to do that one, and I suppose I just didn't want to see it written out in ink on the page. \n\nSo I'm not entirely sure how often it's that specifically, compared to the walking or the fasting. I'm so sorry, I know I should have kept better track for you. Is... is it alright if I don't have the exact answer for that one? I hope I'm not making your job too difficult.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how many times in a month you make yourself sick after eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... um, a whole month? I... I really don't think I could give you a proper estimate, Doctor. I'm so sorry. \n\nLike I was saying, I just put a little check mark when I do *something* to try and make up for the eating. And I really do try to rely on the walking or the yard work instead, because... well, it feels a bit more productive, doesn't it? Less... less shameful. So the times I actually... make myself sick... I try to just push those out of my mind as quickly as possible. \n\nI wouldn't want to give you the wrong number and mislead you. I know you need accurate information to help me. I suppose it happens sometimes, but... I just couldn't say how many times in a month. I do apologize for being so unhelpful with this. Am I... am I making things terribly complicated for you by not knowing?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a large amount of food and then trying to compensate for it happen to you at least once a week for the past three months?\n\nPatient: Well, yes... yes, Doctor. I believe I mentioned that just a moment ago, but I know you have to be absolutely certain for your records. \n\nWhen I look at my little notebook, it does show that for many months now... certainly past the three-month mark... nearly every week has had at least one of those afternoons. You know, where I lose control with the food and then... then feel like I simply must do something to fix it right after, whether that's the fasting, or the chores, or... the other things. \n\nIt's... it's all written down right there in my logs. I hope it's alright that I'm repeating myself, I just want to be sure I'm giving you exactly what you need to hear. Did I answer that properly for you this time?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a large amount of food and then trying to compensate for it happen to you at least three times a week?\n\nPatient: Oh, three times a week? Um... well, let me think for a moment. I... I don't believe it's quite that often, Doctor. \n\nI know my notebook has a mark almost every week, like I said, but... three times in one week sounds a bit... excessive, doesn't it? I really do try my best to keep things under control, and I wouldn't want you to think I'm just completely falling apart every other day. I'm usually quite good at keeping busy with my yard work and staying out of the kitchen. \n\nSo, no, I don't think it reaches three times a week. It's usually just that one bad afternoon, maybe two if it's been a particularly difficult week, but... not three. I hope that's a better answer for you? I really am trying to be as accurate as I can be.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your episodes of eating a large amount of food and then trying to compensate for it have been occurring at the same frequency over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Oh, the frequency... um... well, I suppose it's been fairly steady, Doctor. \n\nI haven't noticed any sudden changes, if that's what you mean. I just try to take each week as it comes and do my best. \n\nSo, yes, I'd say it's been about the same over these past three months. Not getting worse, certainly! I really do try to keep a lid on it. Does that... does that answer your question the way you needed? I want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these episodes of eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh... well... I suppose anyone might feel a little silly after eating more than they meant to, right? \n\nBut guilty... I don't know if I'd use that exact word, Doctor. I mean, I do try to fix it right away, like I said, with the yard work or... the other things, so I make sure it doesn't cause any real harm. I just sort of dust myself off and get back to my day. I wouldn't want you to think I'm sitting around wallowing in misery or anything like that. I like to stay positive!\n\nI just... I just try to handle it quietly and move on. Is... is that a normal way to feel about it? I hope I'm not giving you the wrong impression.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel regret or self-criticism after eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Regret? I try to just push those out of my mind as quickly as possible. \n\nI wouldn't want to give you the wrong number and mislead you. I know you need accurate information to help me. I suppose it happens sometimes, but... I just couldn't say how many times in a month. I do apologize for being so unhelpful with this. Am I... am I making things terribly complicated for you by not knowing?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a large amount of food and then trying to compensate for it happen to you at least once a week for the past three months?\n\nPatient: Well, yes... yes, Doctor. I believe I mentioned that just a moment ago, but I know you have to be absolutely certain for your records. \n\nWhen I look at my little notebook, it does show that for many months now... certainly past the three-month mark... nearly every week has had at least one of those afternoons. You know, where I lose control with the food and then... then feel like I simply must do something to fix it right after, whether that's the fasting, or the chores, or... the other things. \n\nIt's... it's all written down right there in my logs. I hope it's alright that I'm repeating myself, I just want to be sure I'm giving you exactly what you need to hear. Um... well, let me think for a moment. I... I don't believe it's quite that often, Doctor. \n\nI know my notebook has a mark almost every week, like I said, but... three times in one week sounds a bit... excessive, doesn't it? I really do try my best to keep things under control, and I wouldn't want you to think I'm just completely falling apart every other day. I'm usually quite good at keeping busy with my yard work and staying out of the kitchen. \n\nSo, no, I don't think it reaches three times a week. It's usually just that one bad afternoon, maybe two if it's been a particularly difficult week, but... not three. I hope that's a better answer for you? I really am trying to be as accurate as I can be.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your episodes of eating a large amount of food and then trying to compensate for it have been occurring at the same frequency over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Oh, the frequency... um... well, I suppose it's been fairly steady, Doctor. \n\nI haven't noticed any sudden changes, if that's what you mean. It's just sort of... been this way for a while now. I know I mentioned my little notebook, but I don't really tally things up at the end of the month or anything like that. I just try to take each week as it comes and do my best. \n\nSo, yes, I'd say it's been about the same over these past three months. Not getting worse, certainly! I really do try to keep a lid on it. Does that... does that answer your question the way you needed? I want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these episodes of eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh... well... I mean, I do try to fix it right away, like I said, with the yard work or... the other things, so I make sure it doesn't cause any real harm. I just sort of dust myself off and get back to my day. I wouldn't want you to think I'm sitting around wallowing in misery or anything like that. I like to stay positive!\n\nI just... I just try to handle it quietly and move on. I make it right, like I mentioned before, and then I put it out of my mind. I certainly don't sit around calling myself names or getting all critical. I hope that's the kind of attitude you're looking for from me. I want to make sure I'm doing the right thing, you know? Keeping everything neat and tidy. Does that make sense? That just wouldn't feel right to me. If I make a little mistake with my eating, I always make sure to fix it right away, whether that's taking a little something to help things along or... or just skipping my meals the next day to balance the ledger, so to speak. \n\nI like to think of it as just taking responsibility for my actions, you know? I wouldn't want you to think I'm the sort of person who just lets himself go completely. I always try to keep things under control. I hope that's the right way to handle it... is it? I just want to make sure I'm doing what I'm supposed to do.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you ate a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling the need to do something to compensate for it afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, Doctor, I don't... I might sit down and... well, before I even really look up or realize what I'm doing, I can go through a whole loaf of bread. And maybe some peanut butter, and a few cookies... whatever leftovers are in the fridge... and maybe some ice cream, too. All in one sitting. \n\nI know that sounds terrible, and I'm so sorry, I really shouldn't be doing that at my age. Does that sound like what you were asking about, Doctor? I hope I'm not making things sound worse than they are...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating when you have those episodes where you consume a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Well, um... control is a strong word, isn't it? I mean, I try my best to keep things sensible, I really do. But... \n\nI think I mentioned this earlier, and I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, but... yes, that's actually the part that frightens me a bit. Once I start, it's... it's almost like I'm watching myself do it. My hands just keep grabbing for the next thing, the next bite. And the whole time, I'm sitting there in my own head saying, 'Tom, you need to stop this right now, this is ridiculous.' But I just... I can't seem to make myself stop until it's all gone. \n\nI know that must sound so weak, and I'm really embarrassed to even admit it out loud. I should be able to just put the fork down, shouldn't I? I hope you don't think too badly of me for it... is that the sort of thing you meant by losing control?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food interfere with your daily life or cause you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I try very hard to keep it from being a bother to anyone else. I don't want to be a nuisance, you know. But... I suppose, if I'm being entirely honest—and I hope I don't sound like I'm just feeling sorry for myself—it has changed things a little bit. \n\nI used to be quite active with a local garden group, but I've... well, I've sort of pulled away from them lately. And the family meals, too, I've been skipping those. It's just... I find myself either planning my whole day around the food, or I'm just so terribly ashamed afterward. I worry so much that someone will look at me and just... notice what I'm doing, or what I've done. \n\nI feel awful missing out on seeing my family, I really do. I know I should be stronger than this at my age. Does that answer your question, Doctor? I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need...\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen to you regularly?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose \"regularly\" is another one of those tricky words, isn't it? I mean, I certainly don't plan for it to happen often, and I try so hard to keep things normal. But... \n\nI actually started keeping a little notebook, just a small one, to track things. I thought it might help me be a bit more disciplined. But looking at it... well, it seems that for quite a few months now, almost every week has had at least one of those afternoons where things just... slip away from me. \n\nAnd, um... I haven't mentioned this part yet, and I'm so terribly embarrassed to even say it out loud... but after it happens, I just feel so awful, so full and guilty, that I... I try to fix it. Sometimes I force myself to be sick, or I take laxatives... or I just won't eat anything at all the next day. Other times I'll push myself to walk for hours, or do heavy yard chores until I'm exhausted, just to try and undo what I did. \n\nI know it sounds dreadful, especially for a man my age. I should know better, I really should. I'm so sorry to lay all this on you. Is... is that the kind of information you were looking for? I hope I'm not being too much of a bother...\n\nDoctor: How often do you try to fix it after one of those episodes by making yourself sick, taking laxatives, or restricting food the next day?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose it goes right along with what I was just mentioning about my little notebook. I really wish I could say it was just a rare slip-up, I really do. But... when I actually look at what I've written down in the logs, it shows that for many months now... well, nearly every week has had at least one afternoon where I eat out of control, and then... then I try to make up for it right after. \n\nIt's almost always tied together like that. At least once a week, I'll have the episode and then do the vomiting, or take the laxatives, or do the fasting... or push myself to the limit with the walking and the yard chores. \n\nI'm so terribly ashamed to admit that to you. I really thought I was doing better than the notebook says I am. I hope you don't think I'm a completely hopeless case, a man my age acting like this... Is... is that what you needed to know, Doctor? I try so very hard not to do that one, and I suppose I just didn't want to see it written out in ink on the page. \n\nSo I'm not entirely sure how often it's that specifically, compared to the walking or the fasting. I try to just push those out of my mind as quickly as possible. \n\nI wouldn't want to give you the wrong number and mislead you. I know you need accurate information to help me. I'm usually quite good at keeping busy with my yard work and staying out of the kitchen. \n\nSo, no, I don't think it reaches three times a week. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n20\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.2, F50.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hello, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me today, I really appreciate it. I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time. \n\nWell, I'm here because... I've been having a little bit of trouble with my eating lately. I know it sounds a bit silly for an old man to be fussing over food, and I'm sure it's nothing compared to the serious things you usually see, right? But... well, I guess what scares me most is that sometimes, once I start eating, it just feels like my hands keep reaching for more. Even while I’m sitting there telling myself, you know, 'Tom, stop this right now,' it's like I just keep going. \n\nI hope I'm explaining that okay? Is that the sort of thing you can help with?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... guilty? I'm not entirely sure, Doctor. I haven't really thought about it using that word, to be honest with you. \n\nI mean, I suppose nobody likes overdoing things, but... I haven't really noticed a strong feeling of guilt, if that makes sense? I hope I'm answering your question right. I'm sorry if that's not very helpful... did you mean feeling bad about wasting the food, or something else?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose \"large\" is a bit subjective, isn't it? I mean, I certainly don't mean to eat a lot, and I'm sure it's just me being a bit careless sometimes... \n\nBut, yes, I guess on those afternoons when I'm all alone at the house, things can get a little out of hand. I might sit down and... well, before I even really look up or realize what I'm doing, I can go through a whole loaf of bread. And maybe some peanut butter, and a few cookies... whatever leftovers are in the fridge... and maybe some ice cream, too. All in one sitting. \n\nI know that sounds terrible, and I'm so sorry, I really shouldn't be doing that at my age. Does that sound like what you were asking about, Doctor? I hope I'm not making things sound worse than they are...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating when you have those episodes where you consume a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Well, um... control is a strong word, isn't it? I mean, I try my best to keep things sensible, I really do. But... \n\nI think I mentioned this earlier, and I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, but... yes, that's actually the part that frightens me a bit. Once I start, it's... it's almost like I'm watching myself do it. My hands just keep grabbing for the next thing, the next bite. And the whole time, I'm sitting there in my own head saying, 'Tom, you need to stop this right now, this is ridiculous.' But I just... I can't seem to make myself stop until it's all gone. \n\nI know that must sound so weak, and I'm really embarrassed to even admit it out loud. I should be able to just put the fork down, shouldn't I? I hope you don't think too badly of me for it... is that the sort of thing you meant by losing control?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food interfere with your daily life or cause you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I try very hard to keep it from being a bother to anyone else. I don't want to be a nuisance, you know. But... I suppose, if I'm being entirely honest—and I hope I don't sound like I'm just feeling sorry for myself—it has changed things a little bit. \n\nI used to be quite active with a local garden group, but I've... well, I've sort of pulled away from them lately. And the family meals, too, I've been skipping those. It's just... I find myself either planning my whole day around the food, or I'm just so terribly ashamed afterward. I worry so much that someone will look at me and just... notice what I'm doing, or what I've done. \n\nI feel awful missing out on seeing my family, I really do. I know I should be stronger than this at my age. Does that answer your question, Doctor? I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need...\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen to you regularly?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose \"regularly\" is another one of those tricky words, isn't it? I mean, I certainly don't plan for it to happen often, and I try so hard to keep things normal. But... \n\nI actually started keeping a little notebook, just a small one, to track things. I thought it might help me be a bit more disciplined. But looking at it... well, it seems that for quite a few months now, almost every week has had at least one of those afternoons where things just... slip away from me. \n\nAnd, um... I haven't mentioned this part yet, and I'm so terribly embarrassed to even say it out loud... but after it happens, I just feel so awful, so full and guilty, that I... I try to fix it. Sometimes I force myself to be sick, or I take laxatives... or I just won't eat anything at all the next day. Other times I'll push myself to walk for hours, or do heavy yard chores until I'm exhausted, just to try and undo what I did. \n\nI know it sounds dreadful, especially for a man my age. I should know better, I really should. I'm so sorry to lay all this on you. Is... is that the kind of information you were looking for? I hope I'm not being too much of a bother...\n\nDoctor: How often do you try to fix it after one of those episodes by making yourself sick, taking laxatives, or restricting food the next day?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose it goes right along with what I was just mentioning about my little notebook. I really wish I could say it was just a rare slip-up, I really do. But... when I actually look at what I've written down in the logs, it shows that for many months now... well, nearly every week has had at least one afternoon where I eat out of control, and then... then I try to make up for it right after. \n\nIt's almost always tied together like that. At least once a week, I'll have the episode and then do the vomiting, or take the laxatives, or do the fasting... or push myself to the limit with the walking and the yard chores. \n\nI'm so terribly ashamed to admit that to you. I really thought I was doing better than the notebook says I am. I hope you don't think I'm a completely hopeless case, a man my age acting like this... Is... is that what you needed to know, Doctor? I want to make sure I'm answering you properly.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself sick after one of those episodes of eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... um... just the... the making myself sick part? I... I'm afraid I don't know if I can give you an exact number for just that one thing, to be perfectly honest with you. \n\nIn my little notebook, I usually just put a... a little mark when I do *something* to try and fix the eating. I don't always write down exactly which thing I did, because... well, the... the throwing up is the part I feel the most dreadful about. It's so terribly undignified, you know? I try so very hard not to do that one, and I suppose I just didn't want to see it written out in ink on the page. \n\nSo I'm not entirely sure how often it's that specifically, compared to the walking or the fasting. I'm so sorry, I know I should have kept better track for you. Is... is it alright if I don't have the exact answer for that one? I hope I'm not making your job too difficult.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how many times in a month you make yourself sick after eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... um, a whole month? I... I really don't think I could give you a proper estimate, Doctor. I'm so sorry. \n\nLike I was saying, I just put a little check mark when I do *something* to try and make up for the eating. And I really do try to rely on the walking or the yard work instead, because... well, it feels a bit more productive, doesn't it? Less... less shameful. So the times I actually... make myself sick... I try to just push those out of my mind as quickly as possible. \n\nI wouldn't want to give you the wrong number and mislead you. I know you need accurate information to help me. I suppose it happens sometimes, but... I just couldn't say how many times in a month. I do apologize for being so unhelpful with this. Am I... am I making things terribly complicated for you by not knowing?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a large amount of food and then trying to compensate for it happen to you at least once a week for the past three months?\n\nPatient: Well, yes... yes, Doctor. I believe I mentioned that just a moment ago, but I know you have to be absolutely certain for your records. \n\nWhen I look at my little notebook, it does show that for many months now... certainly past the three-month mark... nearly every week has had at least one of those afternoons. You know, where I lose control with the food and then... then feel like I simply must do something to fix it right after, whether that's the fasting, or the chores, or... the other things. \n\nIt's... it's all written down right there in my logs. I hope it's alright that I'm repeating myself, I just want to be sure I'm giving you exactly what you need to hear. Did I answer that properly for you this time?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a large amount of food and then trying to compensate for it happen to you at least three times a week?\n\nPatient: Oh, three times a week? Um... well, let me think for a moment. I... I don't believe it's quite that often, Doctor. \n\nI know my notebook has a mark almost every week, like I said, but... three times in one week sounds a bit... excessive, doesn't it? I really do try my best to keep things under control, and I wouldn't want you to think I'm just completely falling apart every other day. I'm usually quite good at keeping busy with my yard work and staying out of the kitchen. \n\nSo, no, I don't think it reaches three times a week. It's usually just that one bad afternoon, maybe two if it's been a particularly difficult week, but... not three. I hope that's a better answer for you? I really am trying to be as accurate as I can be.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your episodes of eating a large amount of food and then trying to compensate for it have been occurring at the same frequency over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Oh, the frequency... um... well, I suppose it's been fairly steady, Doctor. \n\nI haven't noticed any sudden changes, if that's what you mean. It's just sort of... been this way for a while now. I know I mentioned my little notebook, but I don't really tally things up at the end of the month or anything like that. I just try to take each week as it comes and do my best. \n\nSo, yes, I'd say it's been about the same over these past three months. Not getting worse, certainly! I really do try to keep a lid on it. Does that... does that answer your question the way you needed? I want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these episodes of eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh... well... I suppose anyone might feel a little silly after eating more than they meant to, right? \n\nBut guilty... I don't know if I'd use that exact word, Doctor. I mean, I do try to fix it right away, like I said, with the yard work or... the other things, so I make sure it doesn't cause any real harm. I just sort of dust myself off and get back to my day. I wouldn't want you to think I'm sitting around wallowing in misery or anything like that. I like to stay positive!\n\nI just... I just try to handle it quietly and move on. Is... is that a normal way to feel about it? I hope I'm not giving you the wrong impression.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel regret or self-criticism after eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Regret? Um... well, I try not to be too hard on myself, Doctor. I've always believed there's no use crying over spilled milk, you know? \n\nIf I happen to eat a bit more than I should have, I just... I make it right, like I mentioned before, and then I put it out of my mind. I certainly don't sit around calling myself names or getting all critical. I like to think I keep a pretty level head about these things. At my age, you learn you just have to take things in stride and focus on doing better the next day. \n\nI think it's important to stay positive and not beat yourself up, don't you? I hope that's the kind of attitude you're looking for from me. I really am trying to handle it all as sensibly as I can.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had episodes where you ate a large amount of food in a short period of time without trying to compensate for it afterwards?\n\nPatient: Oh, without trying to make up for it? Um... no, Doctor, I don't believe so. \n\nI mean, I wouldn't just... leave it like that. If I slip up and have a bit too much, I always make sure to take care of it right away. It wouldn't be very responsible of me to just ignore it, would it? I always try to be very diligent about keeping things balanced. \n\nI've never just... let it go. I want to make sure I'm doing the right thing, you know? Keeping everything neat and tidy. Does that make sense? I hope that shows I'm trying my best to stay on top of things.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you ate a large amount of food in a short period of time without doing anything to try and compensate for it afterwards, such as vomiting, taking laxatives, or fasting?\n\nPatient: No... no, I really don't think I have. \n\nLike I was saying, I—I wouldn't just sit there and do nothing after... after overdoing it. That just wouldn't feel right to me. If I make a little mistake with my eating, I always make sure to fix it right away, whether that's taking a little something to help things along or... or just skipping my meals the next day to balance the ledger, so to speak. \n\nI like to think of it as just taking responsibility for my actions, you know? I wouldn't want you to think I'm the sort of person who just lets himself go completely. I always try to keep things under control. I hope that's the right way to handle it... is it? I just want to make sure I'm doing what I'm supposed to do.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you ate a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling the need to do something to compensate for it afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, Doctor, I don't... I don't think I've ever felt quite like that. \n\nI mean, if I've had a bit too much, the feeling that I need to... to set things right, it's always there. It's just a natural reaction, I suppose. It would feel terribly careless of me to just go on with my day and not try to fix it. I wouldn't want to be the kind of person who doesn't care about the consequences, you know? \n\nI always feel that urge to balance things out. It's just my way of being responsible, I think. I hope you can understand that... I just want to make sure I'm keeping everything in order. That's a good thing, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Have you had at least one episode of eating a large amount of food followed by compensatory behavior every week for the past three months?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, I suppose if you look at the—the little food logs I keep, it's been happening about that often. For many months now, really. \n\nAlmost every week there's... there's an afternoon where things get a bit out of hand, and I end up having too much. But I always make sure to fix it! I really do. I'll take care of it right away, either by... well, getting rid of it, or taking something to help it along, or just not eating the next day. Or I make sure to push myself really hard with my walking and the house chores to work it all off. \n\nI just... I want you to know I don't just sit around after it happens. I always try to correct the mistake. That shows I'm trying to be disciplined about it, doesn't it? I hope you don't think too poorly of me for slipping up like that... I really am doing my best to keep it all managed.\n\nDoctor: Have you engaged in both binge eating and compensatory behaviors, such as self-induced vomiting, laxatives, or excessive exercise, at least twice a week for the past three months?\n\nPatient: Oh, twice a week? No, no, I don't think it's quite that frequent. \n\nLike I mentioned, it's—it's usually just that one afternoon a week where things slip a little bit. I really try my hardest not to let it happen more than that. I know I have my moments, but I do try to keep a tight lid on it most days. \n\nI'd be terribly ashamed if I let it get to be twice a week. I mean, once is bad enough, but I'm managing it the best I can, I promise. You do believe I'm trying, don't you? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm completely out of control.\n\nDoctor: Do you engage in binge eating followed by compensatory behaviors, such as self-induced vomiting, laxatives, or excessive exercise, at least once a week?\n\nPatient: Yes, Doctor... um, just like I was trying to say a moment ago, I hope I wasn't being confusing before. \n\nIf you look at my little food logs, they do show that for many months now, nearly every week has had at least one afternoon where I... well, where I ate a bit out of control. But then I always make sure to make up for it, just like you were asking about. I'll fix it by, you know, bringing it back up, or taking a laxative, or fasting the next day, or really pushing myself with my walking and the chores around the house. \n\nIt's just that once a week, though, like I said. I'm really doing my best not to let it be more than that. I hope that makes sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
